===== [p466-468] SECTION 4 HOST-GENERATED PROCESS PROGRAM > Format (20块) =====
--[p467]-- For positive numerals, the sign may be omitted.
(10) If the same parameter is given multiple specifications, the last one takes effect.
(11) The body of a process program must end with two consecutive dollar signs (“\$\$” [24H, 24H]). Any bytes that may follow are ignored. The dollar signs may be omitted if the program consists only of a process program name with no parameters specified.
The parameters constituting a process program are listed in Table 4-1.

Shot Magnification Control (p) (Note 3) | EGAMSW | W : Wafer
N : by P.P. Value
S : Shot
Alignment Method (p) | ALGMTD | 1ST : 1st
EGA :EGA
D/D : D/D (Note 5)
SEA : Search

--[p466]-- then parameter m is assumed to be unspecified.
Note, however, that intermediate spaces within a variable value area are not ignored but handled as follows:
i) In literal data
Space codes are valid characters. For example, data such as:
$$ { \mathfrak { P } } \mathsf { P A R A M E T E R N A M E } = \square \square \mathsf { A } \square \mathsf { B } \square \mathsf { C } \square \square ^ { \mathrm { , } \mathrm { , } } $$
is treated as:
“ABC”
ii) In numerical data
Space codes are treated as zeros. For example, data such as:
--[p466]-- Space codes are treated as zeros.
“\$PARAMETER NAME = 12.3”
is treated as:
“1020.03”
(8) For numerical data, zeros may be used to fill the high-order bits. For example, these three lines mean the same thing:
$$ \ B P A R A M E \mathsf { T E R N A M E } = - 1 2 3 . 4 5 ^ { \circ } $$
$$ \$ 90A9 A M E 7 E R N A M E = -000 123.45 $$
$$ \$ 9 $$
(9) Numerical data may be prefixed with a positive (+) or a negative (-) sign. For positive numerals, the sign may be omitted.

--[p466]-- (4) A period (“.” [2EH]) must be placed between the process name and the program name.
(5) Each parameter name must be preceded by a dollar sign (“\$” [24]H).
(6) An equal sign (“=” [3DH]) must be placed between each parameter name and the following variable value.
(7) Space codes (“” [20H]) are ignored. Therefore, if the entire area of a variable value is filled with space codes, such as:
“\$PARAMETER NAME m = \$PARAMETER NAME m + 1=VARIABLE VALUE m + 1 ...”
then parameter m is assumed to be unspecified.

===== [p210-210] 1.2.1 NSR Receive Messages (2块) =====
--[p210]-- For the above variable-item-length messages, the following IH format is permitted as an exception:
• If the IH length is 0 to 255, the number of length bytes can be 1.
• If the IH length is 256 to 64000, the number of length bytes can be 2.
As an example, a message format for S7F3 is shown below (Table 1-2). (Note 1)
The IH format for the variable-item-length data of this message is “43H.” The IH length permits the following IH format:
– If the IH length is 0 to 255, the IH format can be 41H.
--[p210]-- – If the IH length is 0 to 255, the IH format can be 41H.
– If the IH length is 256 to 64000, the IH format can be 42H.

===== [p7-192] SECTION 1 OVERVIEW (8块) =====
--[p88]-- Specific details of the communications interface are covered in a separate document.
The benefits of online NSR operation include:
• reduction in operator work and, in some cases, the ability to realize unmanned operation,
integrated control of production lot data and reticle data on the basis of Computer Integrated Manufacturing (CIM), and
• feedback and feedforward of process conditions.
This specification is a provisional one and subject to change without notice for purposes of improving equipment functions and providing support for processing parameter modifications.
--[p88]-- This protocol specifies the communication interface required for online wafer processing through transmission of various pieces of information and status data between Step-and-Repeat Systems (NSRs) and the host computer (host), which are electrically connected.
This specification conforms to the Semiconductor Equipment and Materials International (SEMI) Equipment Communication Standard 2 E5-95 (SECS-II).
--[p30]-- HSMS-SS is a subsidiary standard to High-Speed SECS Message Services (HSMS) Generic Services E37-96. It is implemented under the NSR communication control software, NSR/Online Control System-V (NSR/OCSV).
Although the communications contents are not described in this document, they must comply with the SEMI Equipment Communication Standard 2 E5-96 (SECS-II). Specific communications contents are covered in a separate document and are compatible with SECS-I.
--[p30]-- This protocol specifies the communication interface required for online wafer processing through transmission of various pieces of information and status data between Step-and-Repeat Systems (NSRs) and the host computer (host), which are electrically connected.
This specification conforms to the Semiconductor Equipment and Materials International (SEMI) High-Speed SECS Message Services Single-Session Mode (HSMS-SS).
--[p192]-- Part II of the NSR/OCSV Communications Protocol summarizes messages defined by SECS II that are used for controlling the NSR. It also briefly covers the functions of these messages. Detailed information is given in Part III.
Transactions between the NSR and the host are classified into two categories:
--[p7]-- It is implemented under the NSR communication control software, NSR/Online Control System-V (NSR/OCSV).
Although the communications contents are not described in this document, they must comply with the SEMI Equipment Communication Standard II E5-95 (SECS-II). Specific communications contents are covered in a separate document and are compatible with SECS-I.
--[p88]-- It is implemented under the NSR communication control software, NSR/Online Control System-V (NSR/OCSV), which is based on the NSR control software, NSR/MCSV. Hence, the command system of NSR/OCSV is similar to that of NSR/MCSV.
Although the communications interface is not described in this document, it must comply with the SEMI Equipment Communication Standard 1 E4-91 (SECS-I). Specific details of the communications interface are covered in a separate document.
--[p7]-- This protocol specifies the communication interface required for online wafer processing through transmission of various pieces of information and status data between Step-and-Repeat Systems (NSRs) and the host computer (host), which are electrically connected.
This specification conforms to the Semiconductor Equipment and Materials International (SEMI) Equipment Communication Standard I E4-91 (SECS-I).

--[p24]-- 2. This data will be reported as shown below according to the Particle Check Report Format (o) setting.
◆2face:............ Items marked with an asterisk (\*) are not reported. A pound sign (#) indicates that:
• Check mode 1: check area is reticle face
• Check mode 2: check area is pellicle face
• Check mode 3: check area is all faces
◆ 4face (r):........ Items marked with an asterisk (\*) are reported. Therefore, a pound sign indicates that the check area is the reticle face for the items.

===== [p50-50] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > (2) SType = 1: Select.req (1块) =====

--[p338]-- TID:
0: Screen 1 on NSR system console
1: Screen 2 on NSR system console

--[p343]-- 1. HANDLE.......... Handle.
2. READLN.......... Maximum length to read. For a discrete record, the maximum length to read is determined by READLN and RECLEN. The number of records (number of items for FILDAT set in S13F6) is also determined by READLN. However, the maximum size message that can be sent by S13F6 is 32,000 bytes. For a discrete record: number of FILDAT items <= max (1, int (READLN/RECLEN)).
--[p345]-- 1. HANDLE.......... Handle.
2. ACKC13 .......... 0 = OK 3 = ERROR: Illegal Checkpoint value. 6 = ERROR: No open Data Set. 7 = ERROR: Cannot continue. 8 = ERROR: End of data. 10 = ERROR: Pending transaction. 64 = ERROR: Illegal handle. 65 = ERROR: Illegal data set. 68 = ERROR: Illegal maximum length to read. 255 = ERROR: Other error.
CKPNT............ Checkpoint value.
--[p301]-- 1. Refer to Part IV for details.
2. This stream function does not permit a 0 value (request for all constants) for the number of elements.
3. This message is single-block. To request multiple parameters of an ECID that forms a multiple block, a multiple number of single-block requests must be made. (An S2F71 request through a multiblock message will result in an abort transaction reply.)
--[p335]-- 1. The number of parameters requested with S7F81. However, this will be a zero-length list if the process program requested with S7F81 does not exist, the number of parameters requested is zero, or more than 120 parameters are requested.
2. If the specified parameter is not present, the parameter value becomes a zero-length item. Numbers to the right of the decimal place are reported according to the setting in the Floating Data Format (o) parameter (an OCSV system parameter).
The blind ID\* reticle name is reported as the reticle name.
--[p259]-- 1. Indicates the slot number of the reticle currently in the NSR main body. 0 if no reticle is loaded.
2. Indicates the name of the reticle currently in the NSR main body. A space code (20H) is used if no reticle is loaded.
3. Indicates the ID of the reticle currently in the NSR main body. A space code (20H) is used if no reticle is loaded or the reticle ID check has not been performed.
--[p317]-- 1. The process program ID is a 16-character ASCII string determined arbitrarily by the host.
2. This item represents the length, in bytes, of the PPBODY data to be sent in S7F3 (PPS). The length does not include the Item Header.
--[p233]-- Indicates the slot number corresponding to each blind ID.
This will be “0” if a reticle blind ID is not in use.
4. Indicates the reticle name corresponding to each blind ID. A space code (20H) is used if a reticle blind ID is not in use.
--[p228]-- 1. Indicates the number of process programs in the directory. If the number is 6 or more, the message will be multiblock.
2. The data of every IH length is “25 characters” if even one of the process program names (1 through n) has a length of “22 to 25 characters.”
--[p340]-- 1. HANDLE ..........Handle. A short name used to keep track of the state of a particular data set and instance of OPEN between the sending and receiving systems.
2. DSNAME..........Data set name. An ASCII string (format 20) file name that has meaning to both the NSR and the host. It is composed of 1 to 50 alphanumeric characters and the following special characters: $\$ 123,456$ However, using any of these characters: $\$ 123,456,7$ with any of these: \* % in the name will result in an error.
3.
--[p229]-- 1. Indicates the number of reticle types in the list. If this number is 24 or more, the message will be a multiblock message.
2. The data of every IH length is “16 characters” if even one of the reticle type names (1 through n) has a length of “9 to 16 characters.”
--[p269]-- Origin slot information: 0: Wafer not present nn: Wafer loaded from slot “nn” (Valid if origin carrier information is 1 to 3.) 1: Sender carrier information is inline –1: Wafer loaded from unknown slot 4. Wafer presence: 0: Absent 1: Present 5. Lot end signal status: 0: Absent 1: Present 6. Carrier end signal status: 0: Absent 1: Present 7. Wafer lost signal status: 0: Absent 1: Present 8. Empty lot end signal status: 0: Absent 1: Present 9.
--[p323]-- 1. The process program ID is an arbitrarily 16-character ASCII string determined by the host.
2. When the designated PPID resides in the JOB (Identical PPID download) The following restrictions imposed:
Identical PPID with “–” and “(number)” is designated.
Fourteen characters string is valid for PPID. When specify more than fourteen, the fifteenth and sixteenth characters are replaced by “–” and “(number)” respectively.
The PPID with “–” and “(number)” is reported to the host.
--[p319]-- 1. The process program ID is a 16-character ASCII string determined arbitrarily by the host.
2. Represents the length in bytes of the PPBODY data to be sent in S7F3 (PPS). The length does not include the Item Header.
--[p222]-- 1. Indicates foreground processing statuses. See 2 through 13 below.

===== [p290-290] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.15 S2F16 NEW EQUIPMENT CONSTANT ACKNOWLEDGE (MACHINE ADJUSTMENT PARAMETERS) (ECA) (1块) =====
--[p290 [表]]-- The NSR acknowledges, to the host, that the new equipment constant change has been requested (Table 2-44).
Table 2-44 S2F16 Message Format
S, HOST <— NSR
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | Fixed length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | 0210H | S2F16 | W-bit = 0
6~7 | Block No. | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System bytes | System bytes in primary message
Text | 12 | IH format | 21H | binary
13 | IH length | 1
14 | EAC | Equipment acknowledge code (Note 1)
Checksum

===== [p40-40] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > (1) Select Procedure When NSR is in the Active Mode (1块) =====
--[p40]-- The procedure followed by the NSR to establish HSMS communications when it is in the active mode (initiator role) is as follows:
1. The NSR sends the Select.req message to the host.
2. If the NSR receives a Select.rsp with a Select Status of 0, the HSMS Select procedure completes successfully and the SELECTED state is entered.
3. If the NSR receives a Select.rsp with a non-zero Select Status, the Select completes unsuccessfully.
4.
If the T6 timeout expires in the NSR before receipt of a Select.rsp, it is considered a communications failure.

--[p399]-- • ECID IH format and IH length:
There are two IH formats that can be used: 41H and B1H. The IH lengths of these are 6 and 4, respectively.
Here are two entry examples for the IH formats:
• IH format = 41H and IH length = 6:
Possible entry methods for ECID = 12 are:
“12,” “12,” “12,” and “000012”
All of these have the same meaning.
(“” is a space code (20H).)
• IH format = B1H and IH length = 4: Enter an unsigned 4-byte integer value.

===== [p295-295] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.21 S2F22 REMOTE COMMAND ACKNOWLEDGE (RCA) (1块) =====
--[p295 [表]]-- The NSR sends an acknowledgment, to the host, regarding the remote command message (Table 2-50).
Table 2-50 S2F22 Message Format
S, HOST <— NSR
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 13 | Fixed length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | 0216H | S2F22 | W-bit = 0
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System bytes | System bytes in primary message
Text | 12 | IH format | 65H | 1-byte integer (signed)
13 | IH length | 1
14 | CMDA | Acknowledge code (Note 1)
Byte | Item | Data | Description
15~16 | Checksum

===== [p323-323] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.48 S7F71 JOB PROCESS PROGRAM LOAD INQUIRE (PPI) (2块) =====
--[p323]-- Byte | Item | Data | Description
16~31 | PPID | Process program ID (Note 1) (Note 2)
32 | IH format | 71H | Item No. = 2 4-byte integer (signed)
33 | IH length | 4
34~37 | LENGTH | Process program length (Note 3)
38~39 | Checksum
--[p323 [表]]-- The host queries the NSR as to whether it can download a process program to the JOBPP (Table 2- 78).
Table 2-78 S7F71 Message Format S, HOST —> NSR, reply
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 36 | Fixed length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 0
4~5 | Message ID | 8747H | S7F71 | W-bit = 1
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System bytes
Text | 12 | List | 01H | L, 2
13 | No. of elements | 2
14 | IH format | 41H | Item No. = 1 ASCII
15 | IH length | 16

===== [p116-116] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.1.9 Process Program Setting Request (PP Data) (2块) =====
--[p116]-- DIRECTORY: Directory in which process program is stored.
PROCESS-NAME: Process data file name. PROGRAM-NAME: Process program name. PARAMETER-NAME: Name of process program parameter being requested. PARAMETER-VALUE: Value of requested process program parameter.
--[p116]-- (1) Description Reports parameter values set in the process program specified by the host.
(2) Processing Description
Reports the value of the requested parameter for the directory, process, and program specified by the host. If no directory name is specified by the host, the default directory will be searched.
(3) Transaction (Figure 5-12)
(4) Message structure S7,F81 L,4 1. 2. 3. 4. L,n 1. n.
S7,F82 L,n1. n.
DIRECTORY: Directory in which process program is stored.

===== [p17-17] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.7.3 Multiblock Messages (1块) =====
--[p17]-- The system bytes of all blocks of a multiblock message must be the same.

===== [p3-81] Conventions Table (6块) =====
--[p25]-- Notation | Description
Default values areindicated with [Default].Example:◆-999.99 to 9999.99 0[Default]1
(Note 1) | Provides further explanation regarding an operation or term discussed inthe manual.
(*1) | Describes a critical issue concerning an operation or term discussed in themanual.
--[p81 [表]]-- Notation | Description
(m) | Denotes a machine adjustment parameter.
(p) | Denotes a process program parameter.
(s) | Denotes a system parameter.
(c) | Denotes a command parameter.
(c)/(p) | Command parameters that obtain their default settings from a processprogram.
(c)/(s) | Command parameters that obtain their default settings from a systemparameter.
(0) | Denotes an online system parameter.
1 | Denotes a specification range or parameter selection.
--[p3]-- Notation | Description
Default values areindicated with [Default].Example:◆-999.99 to 9999.99 0[Default]1

--[p355]-- 1. The alarm code definitions are as follows:

--[p355]-- 72: Warning on equipment status (extra processing)
Foreground processing: processing of the first command launched from a condition in which no commands are running.
Background processing: processing of the first command launched after initiation of foreground processing (second command to launch from condition where no commands are running).
Extra processing: processing of all commands launched during concurrent foreground and background processing.
2. The alarm ID represents the NSR error code.

===== [p107-141] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > (1) Description (8块) =====
--[p120]-- 2) OCSV-specific commands: STOP command PAUSE command RESUME command PANEL command ASSIST command R-POD command (Note 3) SIGNAL-TOWER command (Note 4)
--[p130]-- The NSR sends the host a report about the occurrence of an error (Figure 5-26). Upon receipt of the error report, it is the job of the host to determine what to do to rectify the situation. The following errors are reported:
• Unrecognized Device ID (S9,F1)
• Unrecognized Stream Type (S9,F3)
• Unrecognized Function Type (S9,F5)
• Illegal Data (S9,F7)
• Transaction Timer Timeout (S9,F9)
• Data Too Long (S9,F11)
• Conversation Timeout (S9,F13)
--[p141]-- The host sends the NSR a Date and Time Request and the NSR responds with Date and Time Data, or the NSR sends the host a Date and Time Request and the host responds with Date and Time Data. In the latter case, the Date and Time Data will be set in the NSR internal clock. This function enables the NSR time base to synchronize with the host time base.
(2) Transaction
a) Date and Time Request From Host (Figure 5-38)
b) Date and Time Request From NSR (Figure 5-39)
--[p127]-- This function enables the NSR to send a report of an event to the host. The event could be measurement results obtained during or at the end of processing execution or processing results produced at the end of processing.
(2) Transaction (Figure 5-23)
(3) Message Structure S6,F11 L,3 1. 2. 3. L,a 1. L,2 1. 2. L,b 1. b. a. L,2 1. 2. L,c 1. c. S6,F12 DATAID: Data ID. CEID: Reporting event ID. RPTID: Report ID. V: Data value.
--[p119]-- Provide the host with the ability to carry out the same type of operations on the NSR that the operator would perform by entering commands at the NSR operation desk.
(2) Transaction (Figure 5-14)

RCMD: Command name. CPNAME: Command parameter name. CPVAL: Command parameter value. HCACK: Host command acknowledge code. CPACK: Command parameter acknowledge code.
(4) Host Commands

===== [p160-185] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > NOTES (10块) =====
--[p185]-- The following command names are reported with the CASCADE command:
a. A command name reported in the event related to job cascade, including job start and job end, is “CASCAD.”
b. A command name reported in the event related to job preparation as the reticle wait process is “JOBRSV.”
c. A command name reported in the event related to job main process as the wafer exposure process is “JOBEXC.”
--[p185]-- \*1 Report at the point when job process has started.
\*2 Report at the point when the job process has completed.
1. A JOBPP is a process program referenced by CASCADE command.
2. When the designated PPID resides in the JOB (Identical PPID download). The following restrictions imposed:
a. Identical PPID with “–” and “(number)” is designated.
b. Fourteen characters string is valid for PPID.
--[p171]-- If S6,F11 is used, a list of available selections is reported by “V: Assist Choice.”
4. If S5,F1 is used, an ALID same as “start assist selection” is reported. For the ALCD, bit 8 (MSB) is set to 0 to show that an alarm condition was cleared.
5. If S6,F11 is used, the selection that was chosen is reported by “V: Assist Choice Result.”
--[p160]-- 1. A CPP is a process program referenced by the EXECUTE Exposure command.
2. An RPP is a process program referenced by the RESERVE Reticle command. It may be downloaded during wafer exposure.
3. The RESERVE Reticle command can be executed whenever the EXECUTE Exposure command is not using the Reticle Loader. This means it can be executed during exposure processing. An alarm report (S5F1) or an event report (S6F11) will notify the host as to whether the Reticle Loader will be used.
--[p185]-- Fourteen characters string is valid for PPID.
When specify more than fourteen, the fifteenth and sixteenth characters are replaced by “–” and “(number)” respectively.
c. The PPID with “–” and “(number)” is reported to the host.
d. Maximum number of identical PPIDs is 10.
3. The maximum number of CASCADE commands to be directed is 50. To direct the multiple number of jobs, “Report Normal Direct Job Execution” or “Report Abnormal Direct Job Execution” have to be received first.
4.
--[p182]-- 1. Issuing a load instruction with the POD locked will result in a POD load operation only. Issuing an unload instruction with the POD locked will result in a POD unlock operation only.
2. During Reticle Loader operation, only the first R-POD command will be accepted; any ones following will cause an unexecutable command error.
--[p160]-- In this example, the RESERVE Reticle command (which uses the Reticle Loader) is launched while the EXECUTE Exposure command (which does not use the Reticle Loader) is executing. The reverse, launching the EXECUTE Exposure command (which does not use the Reticle Loader) while the RESERVE command (which uses the Reticle Loader) is executing, is also possible. Thus, multiple commands can execute at the same time as long as they are not contending for the same resources (such as the Reticle Loader).
--[p171]-- 1. Items in brackets ([ ]) indicate ALIDs that occur during background processing.
2. When S5,F1 is used, different ALIDs are reported depending on the type of assist performed. Assists that occur in the foreground generate ALIDs numbered from 1000 to 1999, whereas assists that occur in the background generate ALIDs numbered from 2000 to 2999. For the ALCD, bit 8 (MSB) is set to 1 to show that an alarm condition occurred.
3.
--[p177]-- 1. Lens magnification calibration is an optional function. It is not supported on NSR scanning systems.
2. This process executes focus calibration for RMPM prior to RMPM lens magnification calibration.
3. A reticle must be loaded on the Reticle Stage to execute measurement using the CALIBRATE Machine command.
4. The above assist (occurring in conjunction with focus calibration measurement) is reported in the following situations:
--[p177]-- The Focus Calibration Judgment (CALIBRATION) (s) parameter is set to “Manual”
The Focus Calibration Judgment (CALIBRATION) (s) parameter is set to “Auto” and the result of auto judgment is “NG.”

===== [p283-283] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.8 S1F9 WAFER TRANSFER STATUS REQUEST (TSR) (1块) =====
--[p283 [表]]-- The host requests that the NSR report the transfer status of all incoming and outgoing wafer ports (Table 2-37). (Note 1)
Table 2-37 S1F9 Message Format S, HOST —> NSR, reply
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 10 | Fixed length (header only)
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 0
4~5 | Message ID | 0109H | S1F9 | W-bit = 1
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System bytes
12~13 | Checksum

===== [p228-228] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.7.5 Reticle Library Assignment Table (RLAT) (SFCD = 4) (2块) =====
--[p228 [表]]-- Responds with reticle names registered in RLAT (Table 2-11).
Table 2-11 Reticle Library Assignment Table (RLAT) (SFCD = 4)
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | 0106H | S1F6 | W-bit = 0
6~7 | Block No.
8~11 | System bytes | System bytes in primary message
Text | 12 | List | 01H | L, 2
13 | No. of elements | 2
14 | IH format | 21H | Item No. = 1 binary
15 | IH length | 1
16 | SFCD | 04H | (SFCD = 4)
--[p228]-- Byte | Item | Data | Description
17 | List | 02H | Item No. = 2 (L, n)
18~19 | No. of elements | n | (Note 1)
IH format | 41H | Item No. = 2.1 ASCII
IH length | 64
Reticle name 1

===== [p94-94] SECTION 3 NSR PROCESSING FUNCTIONS > 3.2.1 Process Programs (3块) =====
--[p94]-- (3) Wafer Alignment Data
(5) Exposure Condition Data
(6) User Option Data
Process programs can be registered and edited using the ENTER Process Program command. Upon registration, a process program is assigned an ID number called the “entry name” (or “process program ID” [PPID]). Once assigned, the entry name can be used for specifying the process program.
--[p94]-- Each PDF is assigned a name, called a “process name,” and each program data is assigned a name, called a “program name.” Normally, product names are used as process names. The combination of the process name and the program name identifies a single “process program.” The program data (hereinafter referred to as process program data, or PP data) are organized into the following six sections:
(1) Wafer Basic Data
(2) Reticle Data
(3) Wafer Alignment Data
(4) Wafer Shot Map Data
--[p94]-- Before wafer exposure processing can be performed on the NSR, a process program must be created. The process program contains exposure processing parameters. The structure of a process program is as follows:
Parameters associated with each step of the process of manufacturing a chip are stored as “program data” (with one set of program data per step and each step corresponding usually to a single layer) in a special file called a Process Data File (PDF).

===== [p98-98] SECTION 3 NSR PROCESSING FUNCTIONS > 3.2.4 Parallel Processing of Commands (2块) =====
--[p98]-- An example of parallel processing is illustrated in Figure 3-4.
In this example, reticle reservation takes place while wafer exposure is being executed. This shortens the time required to load and change reticles.
Wafer exposure could also be started after executing reticle reservation.
--[p98]-- MCSV supports parallel processing of commands, a feature that enables several commands to execute at the same time. This shortens total processing time, thereby improving throughput, by efficiently utilizing idle time for various parts of the machine. However, commands that will affect the operation of the command currently executing may not be used.
An example of parallel processing is illustrated in Figure 3-4.

--[p112]-- (1) Description
The NSR sends a report listing the process data files specified by the host.
(2) Processing Description
The NSR sends a report listing the directory of the process data files stored in the directory specified by the host. If the host does not specify a directory name, the NSR will search the default directory. Wildcards can be used in the process name.
(3) Transaction (Figure 5-8)
(4) Message Structure S7,F73 L,2 1. 2. S7,F74 L,n 1. n.
DIRECTORY: The directory in which the process program is stored. PROCESS-NAME: Process data file name. PROCESSn: Process program list.

===== [p47-48] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > • Messages From the NSR to the Host (2块) =====
--[p47]-- Primary messages sent from the NSR to the host contain system bytes divided into two parts: a source ID and a transaction ID. The distinct source IDs are assigned to each application level originator of the primary message originator.
The transaction ID is set to 0 at the time of system startup and incremented by 1 for each new transaction. When the largest transaction ID has been used, this value starts from 1 (not 0).
--[p48]-- When the largest transaction ID has been used, this value starts from 1 (not 0).
The transaction ID is also incremented by 1 for each request for Select.req and Linktest.req messages.
• Messages From the Host to the NSR
Messages from the host to the NSR are not restricted in these specifications.
• Reply Messages
The System Bytes of a Reply Data Message must be the same as those of the corresponding Primary Message. The System Bytes of a Select.rsp or Linktest.rsp must be the same as those of the respective “.req” message.

--[p10]-- Pins 18 and 25 are optional power supplies for driving external isolation circuits. These pins are not supported.

===== [p211-211] 1.2.2 NSR Send Message (2块) =====
--[p211]-- – If the IH length is 256 to 64000, the number of length bytes will be 2.
As an example, a message format for S6F3 is shown below (Table 1-3). (Note 1)
The IH format of the item length variable data of this message is “02H.” If the number of elements is “1,” the following rule applies:
If the number of length bytes is reported as fixed regardless of the IH length, the IH format will be reported as “02H.”
If the number of length bytes is reported as variable according to the IH length, the IH format will be reported as “01H.”
--[p211]-- For the above variable-item-length messages, the IH format for reporting to the host can be selected by setting system parameters as follows:
Regardless of the IH length, the number of length bytes is reported as fixed (as shown in the details of the message).
• The number of length bytes is reported as variable (depends on IH length):
– If the IH length is 0 to 255, the number of length bytes will be 1.
– If the IH length is 256 to 64000, the number of length bytes will be 2.

===== [p56-56] SECTION 9COMMUNICATION FAILURES (1块) =====
--[p56]-- A communication failure can be caused by any of the following:
• T8 Timeout
• T6 Timeout
• TCP/IP errors
• Host closing the connection
• Incorrect message length
• Incorrect message header
The “incorrect message header” can stem from
– a session ID other than 0xFFFF,
– an SType not supported, or
– a non-zero PType.
If the RJ mode of the NSR Protocol parameter is set to “Response,” however, none of these causes will result in communication failure.

===== [p18-18] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.1.2 Message Blocking (1块) =====
--[p18]-- Message blocking is the division of the message data into blocks to be sent to the Block Transfer Protocol. For best performance, the sender should fill all blocks of a multiblock message, except the last block, with the maximum 254 bytes. The receiver of a multiblock message should be able to accept any block size from 11 to 254 bytes, and should not require consecutive blocks to be the same size.

===== [p48-53] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > Message Format (7块) =====
--[p50]-- Message Length is always 10 (header only).
--[p51]-- Message Length is always 10 (header only).
--[p52]-- Message Length is always 10 (header only).
--[p53]-- Message Length is always 10 (header only).
--[p52]-- Message Length is always 10 (header only).
--[p50]-- Message Length is always 10 (header only).
--[p48]-- The message length is always 10 (header alone) or greater.

===== [p95-97] SECTION 3 NSR PROCESSING FUNCTIONS > Entry Mode (2块) =====
--[p95]-- This mode loads the data to be used for exposure from the Process Program Library (PPL). In this mode, the process program is specified by entering the entry name that has been registered in the PPL.
As shown in Figure 3-3, registered process programs are classified into the following three categories:
1) Current Process Program (CPP) Process programs referenced by the EXECUTE Exposure, CONTINUE Exposure, and READY Exposure commands.
--[p97]-- 2) Reserved Process Program (RPP) Process programs referenced by the RESERVE Reticle command.
3) Process Program Stack (Process Program Library) (PPS) Process programs referenced by the ENTER Process Program command.
CPPs and RPPs are set either by specifying their names or by specifying the PPIDs that are assigned when they are registered in the PPS.

===== [p9-9] SECTION 3 OVERVIEW OF SECS-I (2块) =====
--[p9]-- The SECS-I standard defines point-to-point communication of data utilizing a subset of the international standard known in the U.S.A. as EIA RS-232-C and in Japan as JIS C 6361 for the connector and voltage levels. The actual transmission consists of 8-bit bytes sent serially with one start and one stop bit. The communication is bidirectional and asynchronous, but flows in one direction at a time.
--[p9]-- The communication is bidirectional and asynchronous, but flows in one direction at a time.
The direction is established by special characters and a handshake, after which the data itself is sent. Data is sent in blocks of 254 bytes or less. Each block consists of a 10-byte header followed by data. A message is a complete unit of communication in one direction and consists of 1 to 32,767 blocks. Each block header contains information for identifying the block as part of a specific message. Messages are paired by a request and its reply, which together are called a transaction.

===== [p47-47] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > Header Byte 3 (1块) =====
--[p47]-- This header byte is used in different ways for different HSMS messages. For Control Messages, it contains zero or a status code. For a Data Message whose PType = 0, it contains the SECS Function.

===== [p0-0] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 7.4.1 EXECUTE/PAUSE/RESUME Command Processing Flowchart (Figure 7-8) (1块) =====

===== [p93-93] SECTION 3 NSR PROCESSING FUNCTIONS > (5) UTILITY commands (UTILITY command group) (1块) =====
--[p93]-- The UTILITY commands provide user support functions and NSR system extension functions.
1) Create TERMINAL Emulator command Displays the DEC Terminal Emulator screen, enabling operation of the Digital Command Language (DCL).
2) SHOW Log Data command Displays particle inspection history and other work histories, as well as information on errors that have occurred.
3) Option function commands (Option [UTILITY]) Execute optional functions residing in the UTILITY command group.

===== [p52-52] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > (6) SType = 7: Reject.req (1块) =====
--[p52]-- An HSMS message with SType 7 is sent when the RJ mode of the NSR Protocol parameter is set to “Response.” It is used in response to an HSMS message that is not supported by the NSR or that is not valid at the time.

===== [p107-107] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > d) When the designated PPID resides in the PPS: (1块) =====
--[p107]-- The process program designated as a PPS is changed to a JOBPP. If the host specifies a process program name, the process program is updated and designated as a JOBPP. Part of the data in a process program designated as a JOBPP can be edited at this time.

===== [p58-58] SECTION 10 RELATIONSHIP TO SECS-II > 10.4 STREAM 9 MESSAGES (1块) =====
--[p58]-- Stream 9 messages contain the SECS-II data items MHEAD and SHEAD, which are defined to use a 10-byte HSMS message header.

===== [p124-124] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.4.1 Discrete Variable Data (1块) =====
--[p124]-- (1) Description
Any data report from the NSR that is initiated by an event, such as the completion of a measurement and processing result, rather than the passage of time, is called a discrete variable. This function reports discrete variable data.
(2) Transaction (Figure 5-20)
HOST NSR
S6,F3
Discrete Variable Data Send
S6,F4
Discrete Variable Data Acknowledge
Figure 5-20 Discrete Variable Data Transaction
(3) Message Structure S6,F3 L,3 1. 2. 3. L,n 1. L,2 1. 2. L,m 1. L,2 1. 2. 2. L,2 m.
L,2 1. 2. L,2 n. L,2 1. 2. etc. S6,F4 DATAID: Data ID. CEID: Reporting event ID.
DSID: Data set ID.
DVNAME: Data value name.
DVVAL: Data value.

===== [p8-90] SECTION 2 SCOPE (3块) =====
--[p31]-- These specifications assume that the following software is installed on the NSR:
• NSR/MCSV for controlling NSR operation
• NSR/OCSV for controlling communications between the NSR and the host
While descriptions of these specifications focus on the NSR, they also apply to the (host unless otherwise noted).
--[p8]-- These specifications assume that the following software is installed on the NSR:
• NSR/MCSV for controlling NSR operation
• NSR/OCSV for controlling communications between the NSR and the host
While descriptions in these specifications focus on the NSR, they also apply to the host (unless otherwise noted).
--[p90]-- These specifications assume that the following software is installed on the NSR:
• NSR/MCSV for controlling NSR operation
• NSR/OCSV for controlling communications between the NSR and the host

===== [p133-133] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.8 DATA SET TRANSFERS (1块) =====
--[p133]-- Provides a protocol to transfer data sets between systems. The term data set typically refers to a data structure in a file or in memory. This protocol, however, defines only data structures in a file. Of the data set transfer functions, only one is supported: the host can read a data set residing on the NSR.

===== [p48-48] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > 8.2 HSMS MESSAGE FORMATS (BY TYPE) (3块) =====
--[p48 [表]]-- The specific interpretation of the header bytes in an HSMS message is dependent on the specific HSMS message type as defined by the value of the SType field. The format of messages supported by the NSR is listed in Table 8-4.

===== [p187-187] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 10.2 UNCASCADE COMMAND (1块) =====
--[p187]-- This command is used to stop the process after the current lot process is completed in the case of the multiple job processes.
Even if the specified job’s exposure process has not being started at the point, if the wafer for that job has already been loaded in the NSR, the process for that job is performed. When that job is completed, the job cascade ends.
When this command is directed, the job process will be end after completing the lot process for the current job.
And then all the reserved job information are deleted.

===== [p42-42] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > 7.5 LINKTEST PROCEDURE (1块) =====
--[p42]-- The Linktest is used to determine the operational integrity of TCP/IP and HSMS communications (Figure 7-4). Its use is valid anytime in the SELECTED state.

===== [p53-53] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > (7) SType = 9: Separate.req (1块) =====
--[p53]-- An HSMS message with SType = 9 is used to terminate HSMS communications immediately. No response is given.

===== [p3-81] CONVENTIONS USED IN THIS MANUAL (3块) =====
--[p25]-- The following conventions are used in this manual to simplify descriptions and to provide conformity with the notation used in the documents NSR/MCSV Instruction Manuals and NSR/OCSV Online System Parameter Explanation:

===== [p19-19] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.3.1 Routing Error (1块) =====
--[p19]-- When an NSR receives a block of data which has a device ID in the block header which does not match its own device ID and it has no other knowledge of this device ID, it can assume that the block was sent in error.

===== [p18-18] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.1.4 Interleaving Messages (1块) =====
--[p18]-- Multiple concurrent open transactions are not allowed. Nor is interleaving (concurrently processing) the blocks of different multiblock messages.

===== [p34-34] SECTION 5 OVERVIEW OF HSMS STATES > 5.2.1 TCP/IP NOT CONNECTED (1块) =====
--[p34]-- The entity is ready to listen for or initiate TCP/IP connections but either has not yet established any connections or all previously established TCP/IP connections have been terminated.

===== [p164-165] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > (1) Reticle Reservation (2块) =====
--[p164]-- Reticles can be reserved prior to or during execution of an Exposure command. (See section 8.2, “Detailed Reticle Reservation Transactions,” for details.) Note that when this is done, the following precautions must be observed:
• If a reticle ID check and particle inspection are performed upon reticle reservation, it is not necessary to perform them when the Exposure command executes. In this case, the reticle check result reports explained below in (4) are not sent.
--[p165]-- In this case, the reticle check result reports explained below in (4) are not sent.
• When a reticle reservation is performed during execution of an Exposure command, a request for reticle reservation processing (remote command: RESERVE command) must be sent after the Exposure command finishes using the Reticle Loader (S5,F1; ALID = 301).

===== [p14-14] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.7.4 Receive (1块) =====
--[p14]-- Once a receive state is established, the first byte received is the length byte, N. The receiver counts and saves the following N + 2 bytes. The last two bytes are the checksum. The receiver compares the two checksum bytes against its own computation of the checksum. In a good block, the computed and received checksums are the same.

===== [p42-44] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > (2) Procedure When the NSR is the Responding Entity (2块) =====
--[p42]-- 1. The NSR receives the Linktest.req from the host.
2. The NSR sends a Linktest.rsp.
--[p44]-- 1. The NSR receives the inappropriate message.
2. The NSR sends a Reject.req. The state does not change.

===== [p13-13] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.7.1 Idle State (1块) =====
--[p13]-- Both ends of the communications link are assumed to start in the Idle state. There are two primary activities of the protocol signified by the two exits from the Idle state. These are:
A. SEND — a message block is to be sent.
B. RECEIVE — the other end of the communications link has a message block to send.

===== [p104-107] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > (2) Processing Description (3块) =====
--[p107]-- a) When the designated process program ID (PPID = “entry name”) does not exist a) When the designated process program ID (PPID = “entry name”) does not exist
The process program is temporarily generated and designated as a JOBPP. Part of the data in it can be edited at this time.
--[p104]-- The process program designated as a CPP is designated as a RPP. If the process program name is specified by the host, the process program is updated and designated as RPP. At the same time, part of the data in the process program designated as RPP can be edited.
d) When the designated PPID resides in the PPS
The process program assigned to the PPS is designated as a RPP. If the process program name is specified by the host, the process program is updated and designated as RPP.
At the same time, part of the data in the process program designated as RPP can be edited.
--[p104]-- a) When the specified PPID does not exist
The process program is temporarily designated as a CPP. At the same time, part of the data can be edited.
b) When the specified PPID is the same as the RPP ID
If the process program name is specified from the host, the process program is updated and designated as RPP. At the same time, part of the data in the process program designated as RPP can be edited.

===== [p12-12] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.2.2 Protocol Timeout, T2 (1块) =====
--[p12]-- The Protocol Timeout, T2, limits the time between sending ENQ and receiving EOT, sending EOT and receiving the length byte, and sending the second checksum byte and receiving any character.

===== [p12-12] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.1 HANDSHAKE BYTES (2块) =====

Name | Code | Function
b8 | b7 | b6 | b5 | b4 | b3 | b2 | b1

EOT | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 0 | Ready to receive (04 Hex)
ACK | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 0 | Correct reception (06 Hex)
--[p12]-- EOT | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 0 | Ready to receive (04 Hex)
NAK | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 1 | Incorrect reception (15 Hex)

===== [p14-14] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.7.3 Send (2块) =====
--[p14]-- When the sender receives the ACK before time T2, the block is deemed properly sent.
However, if the sender receives a non-ACK character before time T2, or no character within time T2, it returns to the line control state for a possible retry. In the Send state, characters received prior to sending the last checksum byte can be ignored.
--[p14]-- Once a send state is established, the first byte sent is the length, N, of the data in the block. After N more bytes have been sent, the two bytes of the checksum are sent. The sender computes the checksum based on the data in the block and the block header, but not the length byte. When the sender receives the ACK before time T2, the block is deemed properly sent.

===== [p71-71] SECTION 10 RELATIONSHIP TO SECS-II > GT (Send Grace Timer) (1块) =====
--[p71]-- Specifies the time a SECS message will be stored after the NSR performs a SECS Message Send procedure to the host while the TCP/IP connection is incomplete (Figures 14-9 and 14-10).

===== [p67-67] SECTION 10 RELATIONSHIP TO SECS-II > NOTE (2块) =====
--[p67]-- The “0” remote port number is available only when the NSR is in “Passive” mode.
Specifying “0” will place no restrictions on the host port number.
--[p67]-- 1. The actual usable range is determined by TCP/IP conventions. When the NSR operates in Passive Mode, if “0.0.0.0” is set, it connects to the requested Host without specifying the IP address of the host.
2. The “0” local port number is available only when the NSR is in “Active” mode. Specifying “0” will result in the NSR’s assigning an appropriate number.
3. The “0” remote port number is available only when the NSR is in “Passive” mode.

===== [p20-20] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > NOTES (2块) =====
--[p20]-- MAXIMUM {(Length × T1), 2} (unit: sec.)
T1: inter-character timer
3. The Protocol Timeout (specified as 0.2 sec. in SECS-I) shall have an accuracy of 1 sec.
--[p20]-- 1. For additional information on the Baud Rate range, refer to Section 4.2.
2. While SECS-I prescribes the use of inter-character supervision, one-block supervision (timer supervision from the first character to the last character of the block) shall be used for NSRs conforming to these specifications. The value of the interblock supervision timer is computed according to the following formula:
Interblock supervision timer =
MAXIMUM {(Length × T1), 2} (unit: sec.)
Length: message length

===== [p192-192] NSR-initiated transactions (1块) =====
--[p192]-- The NSR sends a primary message to the host and, if a reply is requested, the host responds with a secondary message to the NSR.

===== [p148-148] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 6.2 HOST MONITORING MODE (2块) =====
--[p148]-- The host monitoring mode is, in effect, a subset of the host control mode discussed later.
The host does not exercise control over the equipment, but instead carries out data collection and monitoring.
--[p148]-- In the host monitoring mode, operations are executed from the NSR Operation Desk. This mode is mostly used for the NSR to send a report; transactions with the host are limited. Specific transactions are described in Part II.
If the NSR receives a primary message from the host for a transaction that is not supported, the NSR replies with an abort transaction message.
The host monitoring mode is, in effect, a subset of the host control mode discussed later.

===== [p148-148] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 6.1 STAND-ALONE MODE (1块) =====
--[p148]-- In the stand-alone mode, the equipment works essentially by itself without communicating with the host. Operations are executed from the NSR Operation Desk. This mode is typically used upon equipment startup, when the host is down, or when there is a communication line failure.
If a primary message is received from the host, the NSR replies with an abort transaction message (Function 0 message).

===== [p35-35] SECTION 5 OVERVIEW OF HSMS STATES > 5.3 STATE TRANSITION TABLE FOR PASSIVE MODE CONNECTION (4块) =====
--[p35]-- No. | PreviousState | New State | Trigger | Action

===== [p19-19] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.2.2 Reply Timeout, T3 (2块) =====
--[p19]-- The Reply Timeout, T3, is a limit on the length of time that the message protocol is willing to wait after the last block of a primary message has been sent and before the arrival of the first block of the reply. If the first block of the reply does not arrive within the T3 limit, the expected block is removed from the list of expected blocks, and the transaction is aborted.
--[p19]-- A timer, called the Reply Timer, is used to measure the time between the last block of the primary message and the first block of its reply. Each open transaction for which a reply is expected requires a separate reply timer.

===== [p41-41] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > 7.3 DATA PROCEDURE (2块) =====
--[p41]-- HSMS data messages may be initiated by either entity as long as the connection is in the SELECTED state. Receipt of a data message when not in the SELECTED state will result in a reject procedure.
Data messages may be further defined as part of a data transaction as either a “Primary” or “Reply” data message. In a data transaction, the initiator of the transaction sends a primary message to the responding entity.
--[p41]-- If the Primary message indicates that a reply is expected, a Reply message is sent by the responding entity in response to the Primary.
The following types of data transactions are supported:
1. Primary Message with reply expected and the associated Reply Message (Figure 7-2).
2. Primary Message with no reply expected (Figure 7-3).

===== [p17-17] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.7.4 System Bytes of NSR-Originated Primary Messages (1块) =====
--[p17]-- The system bytes of a primary message sent from the NSR are divided into two parts: a source ID and a transaction ID. Distinct source IDs are assigned to each application level originator of primary messages in the NSR. The transaction ID is initialized to zero at the time of system startup. It is incremented for each primary message sent. When the largest transaction ID has been used, the transaction ID is set back to 1 (not 0).

===== [p146-146] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.9.8 System Parameter Change Request (2块) =====
--[p146]-- ECV: Equipment constant (set system parameter) value.
EAC: Equipment acknowledge code.
--[p146]-- (1) Description
The host requests that the NSR change specified equipment constants (set system parameters), and the NSR reports the results of the change. These equipment constants (set system parameters) reflect values set using the SET System Parameters command.
(2) Transaction (Figure 5-45)
```csv (3) Message Structure S2,F73 L,n 1. L,2 1. 2. n. L,2 1. 2. S2,F74 ```
ECID: Equipment constant (set system parameter) ID.
ECV: Equipment constant (set system parameter) value.

===== [p402-403] SECTION 3 REMOTE AND HOST COMMANDS > Format (5块) =====
--[p403]-- i) [Required] means that the parameter cannot be omitted.
ii) [Default], [Default: n] (where n = numeral), or [Default: s] (where s = symbol) mean, respectively, the default setting, default value, or default symbol that applies when the parameter setting is omitted.
--[p402]-- For positive numerals, the “+” sign may be omitted.
(6) If the same parameter is specified more than once, the last specification becomes valid.
(7) The command string must end with two consecutive dollar signs: “\$\$” (24H, 24H). Any bytes that may follow are ignored. The dollar signs may be omitted if the command string consists only of a command name, with no parameters specified.
On the following pages, notes in brackets [ ] shown in the “Setting” column have the following meanings:
i) [Required] means that the parameter cannot be omitted.
--[p402]-- Spaces are treated as zeros. For example, data such as:
“\$PARAMETER NAME = 12.3”
is treated as:
“1020.03”
(4) For numerical data, the high-order bits may be filled with zeros. For example, these three lines express the same thing:
“\$PARAMETER NAME = -123.45”
“\$PARAMETER NAME = -000123.45”
“\$PARAMETER NAME = -123.45”
(5) Numerical data may be prefixed with a “+” or a “-” sign. For positive numerals, the “+” sign may be omitted.
--[p402]-- • Format of S2F21
In this message, the following ASCII variable-length character string is specified in the structure: .
“COMMAND NAME \$PARAMETER NAME 1 = VARIABLE VALUE 1 \$PARAMETER NAME 2 = VARIABLE VALUE 2 ... ... \$PARAMETER NAME n = VARIABLE VALUE n\$\$”
(1) Each parameter name must be preceded by a “\$” (24H).
(2) An “=” (3DH) must be placed between each parameter name and the variable value following it.
(3) Space codes (“” = 20H) are ignored.
--[p402]-- (3) Space codes (“” = 20H) are ignored.
Therefore, if the entire area of a variable value is filled with space codes, such as:
“\$PARAMETER NAME m= \$PARAMETER NAME m+1 = VARIABLE VALUE m+1 ...”
then parameter m is assumed to be unspecified. Remember, though, that intermediate spaces within a variable value area are not ignored but instead handled as follows:
i) In literal data
Spaces are valid as characters. For example, data such as:
“\$PARAMETER NAME = ABC” is treated as:
“ABC”
ii) In numerical data

===== [p15-15] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.1 REVERSE BIT (R-BIT) (1块) =====
--[p15 [表]]-- The reverse bit (R-bit) signifies the direction of a message. The R-bit is set to 0 for messages to the NSR and set to 1 for messages to the host. The R-bit is included in the header so that the direction of the message is contained in every block.
Table 7-1 Influence of the R-Bit
R-Bit | Device ID | Message Direction
0 | Destination | Host to NSR
1 | Source | NSR to Host

===== [p39-39] 6.1 TCP/IP NETWORK ADDRESSING CONVENTIONS > (2) Active Mode Connect Procedure (1块) =====
--[p39]-- The procedure followed by the NSR (Active Local Entity) is defined in RFC 793. The procedure is summarized as follows:
1. Obtain a TCP/IP connection endpoint.
2. Initiate a connection to the host remote entry IP address and port specified in the NSR Protocol parameter.
3. Wait for receipt of the acknowledge and the acceptance of the connect request from the host. Receipt of the acceptance from the host indicates successful completion of the connect procedure, and the CONNECTED state is entered.

===== [p13-13] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.7.2 Line Control (4块) =====
--[p13]-- If the retry count does not exceed the value of the RTY parameter, then the Block Transfer Protocol will retry sending the block beginning with ENQ. If the retry count does exceed the value of the RTY parameter, then a failed send has occurred.
--[p13]-- The line control section establishes the transmission direction, resolves contention, and handles retries. When an ENQ is received in the Idle state, the Line Control responds with an EOT if the Block Transfer Protocol is ready to receive. The Block Transfer Protocol then goes to the Receive state. If a message block is to be sent, then an ENQ is sent. If an EOT is received in response to the ENQ within the T2 time limit, the Block Transfer Protocol goes to the Send state.
--[p13]-- If the slave receives an ENQ in response to the ENQ, contention has occurred. The slave postpones the sending of its block until it receives a block from the master. The slave sends an EOT and prepares to receive the incoming block. When the Block Transfer Protocol returns to the Idle state, the postponed block send may be sent as if it were a new send request. After a master sends an ENQ, it can ignore all characters except an EOT.
--[p13]-- After a master sends an ENQ, it can ignore all characters except an EOT.
After a slave sends an ENQ, it can ignore all characters except an ENQ or EOT.
When the time between sending ENQ and receiving EOT exceeds T2, or the time between sending the second checksum byte and receiving any character exceeds T2, or a non-ACK character is received within time T2 (after sending the second checksum byte), the Line Control will increment the retry count.

===== [p42-43] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > (1) Procedure When the NSR is Initiator (2块) =====

===== [p19-19] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.3.2 Duplicate Block Detection (2块) =====
--[p19]-- If the headers are identical, the new block is a duplicate and should be discarded. If the headers are different, the new block is a non-duplicate. The header of the non-duplicate block is saved for comparison with the next block passed on by the block transfer protocol, and the block containing the header is further processed by the message receive algorithm. The NSR can select whether or not to execute duplicate block detection.
--[p19]-- A duplicate block is a block that is exactly the same as the previous block received by the Block Transfer Protocol. This may occur when the receiver has sent an ACK but, for some reason, the ACK did not arrive in time at the sender, causing a send retry. A duplicate block is detected by the SECS-I message protocol by comparing the full 10-byte header of a block currently received by the block transfer protocol with the header of the last block accepted as non-duplicate by the message protocol.

===== [p361-361] SECTION 3 NSR-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 3.12 S6F5 MULTIBLOCK DATA SEND INQUIRE (MBI) (2块) =====
--[p361 [表]]-- If the discrete data report S6F3 (DVS) or S6F11 (ERS) can involve more than one block, this transaction must precede the transmission (Table 3-12).
Table 3-12 S6F5 Message Format S, HOST <— NSR, [reply]
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 26 or 22 | Fixed length (Note 1)

===== [p16-16] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > NOTES (1块) =====
--[p16]-- 1. Single-block messages received by the NSR are an exception; in addition to one, a block number of zero is also acceptable.
2. The maximum block number used by the NSR is 256.

===== [p40-40] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > 7.2 SELECT PROCEDURE (1块) =====
--[p40]-- The Select procedure is used to establish HSMS communications on a TCP/IP connection using the Select.req and Select.rsp messages in a control transaction (Figure 7-1). Select is carried out in the NOT SELECTED state.

===== [p58-58] SECTION 10 RELATIONSHIP TO SECS-II > 10.2 REPLY MESSAGE MATCHING (1块) =====
--[p58]-- When the NSR sends a primary message with a W-Bit = 1 (Reply message expected), it waits for a reply message whose header meets these conditions:
• Session ID matches the value of the primary message session ID.
• Stream matches the stream of the primary message.
The function code number is one greater than that contained in the primary message; otherwise, the reply message function will be zero.
• The system bytes match the system bytes of the primary message.

===== [p13-13] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.5 BLOCK LENGTHS (1块) =====
--[p13]-- The unsigned integer value of the first byte sent after receipt of EOT is the length of the block being sent. The length includes all the bytes sent after the length byte, excluding the 2 bytes of the checksum. The maximum block length allowed by SECS-I is 254 bytes, and the minimum is 10 bytes.

===== [p64-64] SECTION 10 RELATIONSHIP TO SECS-II > SECTION 13 MAXIMUM NUMBER OF SUPPORTED CONCURRENT OPEN TRANSACTIONS (1块) =====
--[p64]-- Processing of multiple concurrent open transactions is not supported.

===== [p165-166] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > (2) Issuing Remote Commands (2块) =====
--[p165]-- Three remote commands can be sent depending on the type of processing to be accomplished: the EXECUTE Exposure command, the CONTINUE Exposure command, and the READY Exposure command. If exposure is executed using the CONTINUE Exposure command, the report described below in (3) is not sent. If exposure is executed using the READY Exposure command, the reports described below in (7) are not sent.
(3) Reticle Processing
A report is sent if reticle processing is performed.
--[p166]-- A report is sent if reticle processing is performed.
(4) Reticle Check Result Report (Upon Reticle Loading)
Reports are sent if a reticle ID check and a reticle particle inspection are performed upon reticle loading.
(5) Reticle Alignment Result Report Reports are sent if reticle alignment is performed.
(6) Reticle Check Result Report (Upon Reticle Unloading) Reports are sent if a reticle particle inspection is performed upon reticle unloading.
(7) Wafer Exposure Processing A report is sent if wafer exposure processing is performed.

===== [p95-95] SECTION 3 NSR PROCESSING FUNCTIONS > 3.2.2 Process Data File (1块) =====
--[p95]-- A PDF is edited using the EDIT Process Data File command. Up to 128 discrete units of program data (see above section) can be stored in a PDF (Figure 3-1).
PDF process names and program names may be a maximum of 12 characters in length.

===== [p16-16] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.4.2 Secondary Message (1块) =====
--[p16]-- A secondary message is defined as any even-numbered message. An even-numbered message will have bit 1 of the lower message ID set to 0.

===== [p38-38] 6.1 TCP/IP NETWORK ADDRESSING CONVENTIONS > (1) Passive Mode Connect Procedure (1块) =====
--[p38]-- The procedure followed by the NSR (Passive Local Entity) is defined in RFC 793. The procedure is summarized as follows:
1. Obtain a TCP/IP connection endpoint.
2. The NSR listens for an incoming connect request from the host to the local entity port specified in the NSR Protocol parameter.
3. Upon receipt of a connect request, quickly acknowledge it and indicate acceptance of the connection. At this point, the connect procedure has completed successfully, and the CONNECTED state is entered.

===== [p403-403] SECTION 3 REMOTE AND HOST COMMANDS > Structure: L, 2 (1块) =====
--[p403]-- 1. (1)
2. L,n (n represents the number of parameters)

===== [p321-321] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > Process data format (1块) =====
--[p321]-- “Process\_data\_file\_name.Process\_data\_name\$\$”
(1) The process data file name is an ASCII string of 12 characters or less.
(2) The process data name is an ASCII string of 12 characters or less.
(3) A period (“.” [2EH]) must be placed between the process data file name and the process data name.
(4) The process data must end with two consecutive dollar signs (“\$\$” [24H, 24H]).

===== [p101-101] SECTION 4 INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 4.9 OTHER FUNCTIONS (1块) =====
--[p101]-- Other functions include loop-back diagnostic services for communication line checking, date/time and equipment constant requests. These functions are implemented in Stream 2 transactions.

===== [p43-43] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > 7.6 SEPARATE PROCEDURE (1块) =====
--[p43]-- The Separate procedure is used to abruptly terminate HSMS communication for an entity prior to breaking the TCP/IP Connection. HSMS requires that the connection be in the SELECTED state when using Separate. When the NSR is the responding entity, it does not send a response and is required to terminate communications regardless of its local state (Figure 7-5).

===== [p102-102] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.1.1 Process Program Download (1块) =====
--[p102]-- This function lets the host select (or specify) and register in the NSR a process program required to execute a process.

===== [p44-44] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > NOTE (3块) =====
--[p44]-- 1. Receipt of the Reject.req will always cause communications to fail regardless of the status of the Reject Response (RJ) mode of the NSR Protocol parameter.
--[p44]-- • A Select.rsp message is received when the NSR is not waiting for one.
• An unsupported SType message is received.
• The NSR receives a message with a non-zero PType.
• A Linktest.req message is received in a state other than SELECTED.
• A Linktest.rsp is received when the NSR is not waiting for one.
--[p44]-- 1. Setting the RJ mode of the NSR Protocol parameter to “Response” will result in execution of the sending Reject.req procedure; setting it to “No Response” will cause communications to fail.
The Reject procedure will execute in any of these cases:
• A data message is received in the NOT SELECTED state.
• A Select.req message is received in the Active mode in the NOT SELECTED state.
• A Select.rsp message is received when the NSR is not waiting for one.

===== [p15-15] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE (1块) =====
--[p15]-- The operation of all communications functions above the block transfer protocol is linked to information contained in a 10-byte data element called the header (Figure 7-1).
Figure 7-1 Block Header Structure
8 7 6 5 4 3 2 1
1 | R | Upper Device ID
2 | Lower Device ID | Stream No.
3 | W | Upper Message ID
4 | Lower Message ID | Function No.
5 | E | Upper Block No.
6 | Lower Block No.
7 | System Byte System Byte | Upper Source ID Lower Source ID
8
9 | System Byte | Upper Transaction ID
10 | System Byte | Lower Transaction ID

===== [p46-46] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > (3) Message Length (1块) =====
--[p46]-- Message Length is a four-byte unsigned integer value which specifies the length, in bytes, of the Message Header plus the Message Text. Message Length is transmitted most significant byte (MSB) first and least significant byte (LSB) last.
The minimum possible Message Length is 10 (Header only).

===== [p47-47] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > Session ID (1块) =====
--[p47]-- Control messages (Select.req, Select.rsp, Linktest.req, Linktest.rsp, and Separate.req) are always 0xFFFF in binary, all ones. Device IDs are specified in the NSR Protocol parameter.

===== [p11-11] SECTION 4 COUPLING > 5.1 CHARACTER (2块) =====
--[p11 [表]]-- The 8 data bits may be any arbitrary code.
The eight data bits will hereafter be referred to as a byte.
Figure 5-1 Character Structure
Start Bit | 6 | MSB 7 8 | Stop Bit
LSB 1 2 | 3 | 4 | 5
0 | Time | 1 →
--[p11]-- Data shall be transmitted or received in a serial bit stream of 10 bits per character at one of the specified data rates. The standard character has one start bit (0), 8 data bits, and one stop bit (1). All bit transmissions are of the same duration. The 8 data bits are numbered from 1 to 8 in the order sent (Figure 5-1). The timing between characters is asynchronous with respect to the data rate. The 8 data bits may be any arbitrary code.

===== [p46-46] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > (1) Byte Structure (1块) =====
--[p46]-- Within this standard, a byte contains eight (8) bits. The bits in a byte are numbered from Bit 7 (most significant) to Bit 0 (least significant).

===== [p54-54] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > 8.3 HSMS SCENARIO (1块) =====
--[p54]-- A scenario (Table 8-8) has been provided to illustrate the HSMS procedures used for a typical session.

===== [p18-18] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.1.1 Message Length (1块) =====
--[p18]-- The maximum data length in a single block of a message is 244 bytes. The maximum number of blocks that can be sent in a multiblock message is 256, and so the maximum data length allowed in one message is 244 × 256 bytes.

===== [p128-128] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.5.1 Alarm Report (1块) =====
--[p128]-- (1) Description
The NSR sends the host a report about the occurrence or clearing of an alarm condition on the NSR. This report is issued whenever an alarm is set or cleared. There is no corresponding clear message for irrecoverable errors and equipment status warning errors.
(2) Transaction (Figure 5-24)
(3) Message Structure
S5,F1 L,3 1. 2. 3. S5,F2
ALCD: Alarm (category) code.
ALID: Alarm ID (NSR error number).
ALTX: Alarm text.
ACKC5: Acknowledge code.

===== [p148-148] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 6.3 HOST CONTROL MODE (1块) =====
--[p148]-- In the host control mode, the NSR is controlled and operated by the host. This essentially eliminates the need to perform operations from the NSR Operation Desk. This mode supports all transactions contained in Part II of this specification.

===== [p16-16] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.3 WAIT BIT (W-BIT) (1块) =====
--[p16]-- The Wait bit (W-bit) is used to indicate that the sender of a primary message expects a reply. A value of one in the W-bit means that a reply is expected. A value of zero in the W-bit means that no reply is expected. The W-bit must be set to zero in all secondary messages. For multiblock messages, the sender must ensure that the W-bit is the same in every block of the message.

===== [p351-351] SECTION 3 NSR-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 3.4 S2F17 DATE AND TIME REQUEST (DTR) (1块) =====
--[p351 [表]]-- The NSR requests that the host send the date and time to establish synchronization between the NSR time base and the host time base (Table 3-4).
Table 3-4 S2F17 Message Format
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 10 | Fixed length (header only)
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | 8211H | S2F17 | W-bit = 1
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System byte
12~13 | Checksum
S, HOST <— NSR, reply

===== [p18-18] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.2.1 Reply Linking (2块) =====
--[p18]-- The expected block will have the complement of the R-bit, will have the same device ID, will be the first block of a secondary message, and will have the same system bytes as those of the given primary message.
--[p18]-- When a reply is expected for a primary message, the message protocol starts the reply timer for the transaction after the last block of the message is successfully sent. When a primary message is sent for which a reply is requested, an expected block is established for the message receive algorithm.

===== [p107-107] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > c) When the designated PPID is the same as an RPP ID (1块) =====
--[p107]-- The process program designated as an RPP is designated as a JOBPP. If the host specifies a process program name, the process program is updated and designated as a JOBPP. Part of the data in a process program designated as a JOBPP can be edited at this time.

===== [p39-39] 6.1 TCP/IP NETWORK ADDRESSING CONVENTIONS > NOTES (2块) =====
--[p39]-- The connection procedure will take place and, when complete, the CONNECTED state will be entered. An attempt to establish HSMS communications will fail in the Select procedure, however, causing the TCP/IP connection to close and the passive mode connect procedure to start over from the beginning.
--[p39]-- 1. Rejection of Additional Connection Requests
When it receives multiple connection requests, the NSR will refuse to listen for or accept connection requests from hosts other than the first one it connects to.
2. Rejection of Connection Requests From Unspecified Hosts
Upon receipt of a connect request from a host at a remote entity IP address not specified in the NSR Protocol parameter, the NSR will indicate acceptance of the request.

===== [p0-0] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 7.2.1 Processing Flowchart for READY Exposure and EXECUTE Exposure Command Execution (Figure 7-3) (1块) =====
--[p0]-- Figure 7-3 Processing Flowchart for READY Exposure and EXECUTE Exposure Command Execution

===== [p14-14] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.7.5 Receive Completion (2块) =====
--[p14]-- After a block is correctly received, an ACK character is sent and the message protocol is notified that a block has been received. If T2 is exceeded while waiting for the length character, or T1 is exceeded between characters being received, then a NAK is sent. If the length byte is invalid or if the received checksum does not agree with the computed checksum, the receiver continues to listen for characters to ensure that the sender is finished sending.
--[p14]-- This is detected when the inter-character time exceeds T1, at which point the receive is aborted and a NAK is sent. In any case, when a NAK is sent, the receiver may discard any data it has received for the block. The receiver returns immediately to the Idle state after sending an ACK or NAK.

===== [p315-315] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.40 S7F19 PROCESS PROGRAM LIST REQUEST (PP ENTRY) (RER) (1块) =====

===== [p102-102] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > CPP Download (3块) =====
--[p102]-- Part of the data in a process program designated as a CPP can be edited at this time.
d) When the designated PPID resides in the PPS:
The process program designated as a PPS is changed to a CPP. If the host specifies a process program name, the process program is updated and designated as a CPP. Part of the data in a process program designated as a CPP can be edited at this time.
--[p102]-- (1) Description
This function is used by the host to instruct the NSR to select (specify) and edit a Current Process Program (CPP; a process program referenced by the EXECUTE, CONTINUE, or READY Exposure commands).
(2) Processing Description
a) When the designated process program ID (PPID = “entry name”) does not exist The process program is temporarily generated and designated as a CPP. Part of the data in it can be edited at this time.
b) When the specified PPID is the same as the CPP ID
--[p102]-- If the process program to be downloaded from the host is specified with a process program name, the process program is updated and designated as a CPP. Part of the data in a process program designated as a CPP can be edited at this time.
c) When the designated PPID is the same as an RPP ID
The process program designated as an RPP is designated as a CPP. If the host specifies a process program name, the process program is updated and designated as a CPP.

===== [p40-40] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES (1块) =====
--[p40]-- Once the TCP/IP connection is established, the two entities establish HSMS communication with the Select procedure. Then data messages may be exchanged in either direction at any time. When the entities wish to end HSMS communications, the Separate procedure is used to end HSMS communications.

===== [p0-0] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 7.1.2 Typical Transaction Sequence in EXECUTE Exposure Command Execution (Figure 7-2) (1块) =====
--[p0]-- Figure 7-2 Typical Transaction for EXECUTE Exposure Command

===== [p40-40] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > 7.1 SENDING AND RECEIVING HSMS MESSAGES (1块) =====
--[p40]-- All HSMS procedures involve the exchange of HSMS messages. These messages are sent and received as TCP/IP streams using the previously established TCP/IP connection.

===== [p33-33] SECTION 4 HSMS OVERVIEW (1块) =====
--[p33]-- High-Speed SECS Message Services (HSMS) defines a communication interface suitable for the exchange of messages between the NSR and the host in a semiconductor factory using a TCP/IP environment. HSMS uses TCP/IP stream support, which provides reliable, two-way simultaneous transmission of streams of contiguous bytes.

===== [p114-114] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.1.7 Process Program Registered Reticle Name Request (2块) =====
--[p114]-- (1) Description
The NSR reports the reticle name registered in the process program specified by the host.
(2) Processing Description
The NSR sends a report listing the reticle names registered in the directory, process, and program specified by the host. If the directory specified by the host does not exist, it will search the default directory.
(3) Transaction (Figure 5-10)
Figure 5-10 Transaction for Process Program Registered Reticle Name Request

===== [p272-272] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > NOTE > 1. Reticle SMIF POD status: (1块) =====
--[p272]-- –1: No indexer
0: No reticle POD

4: Reticle not ready for loading from reticle POD

===== [p321-321] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > Process program save operation (1块) =====
--[p321]-- The process program corresponding to the PPID specified by the host is saved in a process data file. This process data file is the one specified in PDDATA by the host. In the process data file, the process program is saved with the name specified in PDDATA by the host.

===== [p260-260] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 1. Specified lot ID: (1块) =====
--[p260]-- If the lot ID is not specified, or the lot is finished, reporting will be done using a space code (20H).
If the process program has been downloaded, the lot ID is cleared and reporting will be done using a space code (20H).

===== [p51-51] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > (4) SType = 5: Linktest.req (1块) =====
--[p51]-- An HSMS message with SType 5 is a “Linktest Request” Control Message.

===== [p16-16] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.4.1 Primary Message (1块) =====
--[p16]-- A primary message is defined as any odd-numbered message. An odd-numbered message will have bit 1 of the lower message ID set to 1.

===== [p38-38] 6.1 TCP/IP NETWORK ADDRESSING CONVENTIONS > 6.1.1 IP Addresses (1块) =====
--[p38]-- IP addresses are outside the scope of the HSMS protocol. For additional information, refer to the relevant section of RFC 791 of the Internet Protocol (IP).

===== [p363-363] SECTION 3 NSR-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 3.14 S6F11 EVENT REPORT SEND (ERS) (3块) =====
--[p363 [表]]-- The purpose of this message is for the NSR to send a defined, linked, and enabled group of reports to the host upon the occurrence of an event (CEID). If this message is multiblock, it must be preceded by the S6F5/S6F6 inquire/grant transaction (Table 3-14).

===== [p49-49] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > • Header Byte 2 (2块) =====
--[p49]-- The low-order 7 bits (bits 6–0) of Header Byte 2 contain the SECS Stream for the message.
The Stream is a 7-bit unsigned integer value, which identifies a major topic of the message (Table 8-5).
Table 8-5 Header Byte 2 Format
7 6 0
W-Bit | Stream
--[p49]-- The most significant bit (bit 7) of Header Byte 2 is the W-Bit. In a Primary Message, the W-Bit indicates whether the Primary Message expects a Reply message. A Primary Message which expects a Reply should set the W-Bit to 1. A Primary Message which does not expect a Reply should set the W-Bit to 0. A Reply Message should always set the W-Bit to 0. The low-order 7 bits (bits 6–0) of Header Byte 2 contain the SECS Stream for the message.

===== [p16-16] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.6 BLOCK NUMBER (1块) =====
--[p16]-- A message sent as more than one block is called a multiblock message. The first block is given a block number of one, and the block number is incremented by one for each subsequent block until the entire message is sent. The blocks of a multiblock message are sent in order. In a single-block message, the block number must have a value of one. The maximum block number is 32,767.

===== [p369-369] SECTION 3 NSR-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 3.22 S9F13 CONVERSATION TIMEOUT (CTN) (2块) =====
--[p369 [表]]-- The NSR informs the host that the next transaction message expected from the host was not received within a reasonable length of time. CTN is a timeout error for the next transaction, whereas TTN is a timeout error for a secondary message (Table 3-22).
