===== [p16-16] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.5 END BIT (E-BIT) (1块) =====
--[p16]-- The End bit (E-bit) is used to determine if a block is the last block of a message. A value of one in the E-bit means that the block is the last block. A value of zero means that more blocks are to follow.

===== [p17-17] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.7.1 Distinction (1块) =====
--[p17]-- The system bytes of a primary message must be distinct from those of all currently open transactions initiated from the same end of the communications link. They must also be distinct from those of the most recently completed transaction. They must also be distinct from any system bytes of blocks that were not successfully sent since the last successful block send.

===== [p49-49] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > • Header Byte 3 (2块) =====
--[p49]-- Header Byte 3 contains the SECS Function for the message. The Function is an 8-bit unsigned integer value. The least significant bit (bit 0) of the Function defines whether the Data Message is Primary or Reply; the value 1 indicates Primary and the value 0 indicates Reply.
• PType
Set to 0.
• SType
Set to 0.
• System Bytes
For a Primary Message, System Bytes contain a value uniquely identifying this transaction from all other open transactions.
--[p49]-- For a Reply Message, System Bytes contain the same value as the corresponding Primary Message.
The HSMS Message Text will be formatted as SECS-II messages.

===== [p19-19] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.3.3 Interblock Timeout, T4 (1块) =====
--[p19]-- The time interval between the successful receipt of a block in a multiblock message, and the successful receipt of the subsequent block of the same message, is limited to time T4. If this time is exceeded, the message is canceled and the transaction is aborted. A time, called the Interblock Timer, is used to measure the time between block arrivals in the message protocol.

===== [p22-22] HSMS Interface Specifications (1块) =====
--[p22]-- Installation Preparation Manual
Revision 2.0
NIKON CORPORATION

===== [p41-41] SECTION 7MESSAGE EXCHANGE PROCEDURES > (2) Select Procedure When NSR is in the Passive Mode (1块) =====
--[p41]-- The procedure followed by the NSR to establish HSMS communications when it is in the passive mode (responding entity role) is as follows:
1. The NSR receives the Select.req message.
2. If the NSR is able to accept the select, it transmits the Select.rsp with a Select Status of 0. The HSMS Select procedure for the NSR completes successfully and the SELECTED state is entered.
3. If the NSR is unable to permit the select, it transmits the Select.rsp with a non-zero Select Status.
The HSMS Select procedure for the NSR completes unsuccessfully (no state transactions).

3) STOP command
Forces reticle or wafer processing to terminate.

===== [p39-39] 6.1 TCP/IP NETWORK ADDRESSING CONVENTIONS > 6.3 TERMINATING A TCP/IP CONNECTION (1块) =====
--[p39]-- Termination of a connection is permitted only when HSMS is in the TCP/IP CONNECTED state. At this time, when conditions for termination of the TCP/IP connection occur, the TCP/IP connection is immediately terminated. The procedures for termination of a connection are defined in RFC 793.

===== [p0-0] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 7.5.2 Typical Transaction Sequence for Parallel Processing in EXECUTE Exposure and RESERVE Reticle Commands (Figure 7-11) (1块) =====
--[p0]-- Figure 7-11 Typical Transaction for Parallel Processing of EXECUTE Exposure and RESERVE Reticle Commands

===== [p260-260] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2. Command status: (1块) =====
--[p260]-- 0: Command not executed
1: Command execution in progress
In this case, command refers to the EXECUTE, CONTINUE, or READY Exposure command or CASCADE command, which can specify a lot ID.

===== [p34-34] SECTION 5 OVERVIEW OF HSMS STATES > HSMS Selected (1块) =====
--[p34]-- A substate of CONNECTED in which at least one HSMS session has been established. This is the normal “operating” state of HSMS: data messages may be exchanged in this state.

===== [p286-286] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.11 S1F68 COMMUNICATION MODE SWITCH ACKNOWLEDGE (CMCA) (1块) =====
--[p286 [表]]-- The NSR acknowledges, to the host, that the online communication mode switch request has been received (Table 2-40).
Table 2-40 S1F68 Message Format S, HOST <— NSR
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 13 | Fixed length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 0
4~5 | Message ID | 8144H | S1F68 | W-bit = 1
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System bytes
Text | 12 | IH format | 65H | 1-byte integer (signed)
13 | No. of elements | 1
14 | COMMODACK | Acknowledge code (Note 1)
Byte | Item | Data | Description
15~16 | Checksum

===== [p38-38] 6.1 TCP/IP NETWORK ADDRESSING CONVENTIONS > 6.1.2 TCP Port Numbers (1块) =====
--[p38]-- HSMS implementations should allow configuring the TCP Port to the full range of the TCP/IP implementation used. A typical TCP Port Number is 5000. Conventions have been established for selecting TCP Port Numbers which are outside the scope of the HSMS protocol. For additional information, refer to RFC 793.

===== [p315-322] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > NOTE > 1. ACKC7: (2块) =====
--[p315]-- 0: Successfully deleted
1: Not granted because initialization or resetting is in progress
2: Not granted
4: PPID not defined
64: Not granted because currently in use.
65: Invalid PPID
66: Wrong number of elements
67: Not granted under current operator name
128: Internal system error
--[p322]-- 0: Successfully saved
2: There is no space to save
3: Wrong PPID
4: Not granted because initialization or resetting is in progress
64: Not granted because it is now in use
65: Not granted under the current operator name
66: PPID not registered
72: Parameter setting error
73: Process data string format error
76: Wrong program name or wrong process name
128: Internal system error

===== [p101-101] SECTION 4 INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 4.8 DATA SET TRANSFERS (1块) =====
--[p101]-- This function enables the host to read the data set from the equipment. This function is implemented in Stream 13 transactions.

===== [p353-353] SECTION 3 NSR-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 3.7 S2F26 LOOPBACK DIAGNOSTIC DATA (LDD) (1块) =====
--[p353 [表]]-- The host echoes back the diagnosis message received in S2F25 (LDR) (Table 3-7).
Table 3-7 S2F26 Message Format
S, HOST —> NSR
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 13+n | Variable length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 0
4~5 | Message ID | 021AH | S2F26 | W-bit = 0
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System byte | System byte in primary message
Text | 12 | IH format | 22H | binary
13~14 | IH length | n | n ≤ 241
15~14+n | ABS | Echoed binary string (Note 1)
Byte | Item | Data | Description
15+n~16+n | Checksum

===== [p368-368] SECTION 3 NSR-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 3.21 S9F11 DATA TOO LONG (DLN) (1块) =====
--[p368 [表]]-- This message to the host indicates that the NSR has been sent more data than it can handle (Table 3-21).
Table 3-21 S9F11 Message Format
S, HOST <— NSR
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 22 | Fixed length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | 090BH | S9F11 | W-bit = 0
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System byte
Text | 12 | IH format | 21H | binary
13 | IH length | 10
14~23 | MHEAD | Header of message with error
24~25 | Checksum

===== [p214-214] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.2 S1F1 ARE YOU THERE REQUEST (R) (1块) =====
--[p214 [表]]-- Establishes whether the NSR is online. If the NSR is offline (stand-alone mode), a Function 0 response is used to abort the transaction. If the NSR is powered down, the host detects it through the expiration of a timeout timer (Table 2-2).
Table 2-2 S1F1 Message Format
S, HOST —> NSR, reply
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 10 | Fixed length (header only)
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 0
4~5 | Message ID | 8101H | S1F1 | W-bit = 1
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
Byte | Item | Data | Description
8~11 | System byte
12~13 | Checksum

===== [p306-306] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.31 S5F4 ENABLE/DISABLE ALARM ACKNOWLEDGE (EAA) (1块) =====
--[p306 [表]]-- The NSR acknowledges, to the host, that alarm sending is disabled (Table 2-60).
Table 2-60 S5F4 Message Format
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 13 | Fixed length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | 0504H | S5F4 | W-bit = 0

ACKC5: 0: Successfully received 65: Received undefined ALID 66: Received undefined ALCD 67\~71: (Reserved)

===== [p107-107] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > e) When the designated PPID resides in the JOB (Identical PPID download) (1块) =====
--[p107]-- The following restrictions imposed:
Identical PPID with “–” and “(number)” is designated.
Fourteen characters string is valid for PPID. When specify more than fourteen, the fifteenth and sixteenth characters are replaced by “–” and “(number)” respectively.
\- The PPID with “–” and “(number)” is reported to the host.
Maximum number of identical PPIDs is 10.

===== [p58-58] SECTION 10 RELATIONSHIP TO SECS-II > 10.3 T3 REPLY TIMEOUT (1块) =====
--[p58]-- The T3 reply timeout is a limit on the length of time that the HSMS message protocol is willing to wait for a reply message.
After sending a primary message with a W-bit 1 (reply expected), the NSR must begin a reply timer, initialized to the T3 value. If the NSR does not receive the reply message before the reply timer expires, then a T3 Timeout Error has occurred. The NSR closes the transaction and no longer expects the reply message.

===== [p360-360] SECTION 3 NSR-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 3.11 S6F4 DISCRETE VARIABLE DATA ACKNOWLEDGE (DVA) (1块) =====
--[p360 [表]]-- The host responds to the NSR with an acknowledgment or error (Table 3-11).

--[p13]-- The master/slave parameter is used in the resolution of contention. The host is designated as the slave. The NSR is designated as the master.

===== [p93-93] SECTION 3 NSR PROCESSING FUNCTIONS > (6) SYSTEM commands (SYSTEM command group) (1块) =====
--[p93]-- The SYSTEM commands are used for MCSV system management.
1) SET System Parameters command Sets system parameters.
2) ADJUST Machine Parameters command Sets machine adjustment parameters.
3) AUTHORIZE Operator command Registers operator names and corresponding operation environments.
4) RESET System command
Initializes MCSV and specified controllers.
5) INITIALIZE System command Initializes MCSV and all the controllers.
6) SHUTDOWN System command Shuts down MCSV.

===== [p46-46] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > (2) Message Format (1块) =====
--[p46 [表]]-- An HSMS Message is transmitted as a single contiguous stream of bytes in the following order (Table 8-1):
Table 8-1 HSMS Message Format
Number of Bytes | Description
4 Bytes | Message Length. MSB First. Specifies the number of bytesin the Message Header plus the Message Text.
10 Bytes | Message Header
0-n Bytes | Message Text. Format is further specified by PType field ofmessage header.

===== [p34-34] SECTION 5 OVERVIEW OF HSMS STATES > 5.2.2 TCP/IP CONNECTED (1块) =====
--[p34]-- A TCP/IP connection has been established. This state has two substates, NOT SELECTED and SELECTED.

===== [p47-47] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > System Bytes (1块) =====
--[p47]-- System Bytes is a four-byte field occupying header bytes 6–9. System Bytes is used to identify a transaction uniquely among the set of open transactions.

===== [p15-15] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.2 DEVICE ID (1块) =====
--[p15]-- The device ID defines the source or destination of the message, depending upon the value of the Rbit (as shown in Table 7-1). Device identification is a property of the NSR and must be settable for each one. The host can view the device ID as a logical identifier connected with a physical device within the NSR. The host has no device ID.

===== [p192-192] Host-initiated transactions (1块) =====
--[p192]-- The host sends a primary message to the NSR and, if a reply is requested, the NSR responds with a secondary message to the host.

===== [p18-18] SECTION 8 MESSAGE PROTOCOL > 8.2 TRANSACTIONS (1块) =====
--[p18]-- A transaction is a primary message and an optional corresponding secondary message called the reply. A transaction is opened when a primary message is ready to be sent. A transaction is closed when the last block of a primary message requesting no reply has been sent, or when the last block of the reply has been received.

===== [p342-342] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2. RTYPE .............Record type (1块) =====
--[p342]-- The Record Type determines the way the data set is divided into messages for transfer to the receiving system. There are two types of records: Discrete and Stream. In these specifications, however, only discrete records are supported.

===== [p123-123] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.3.3 Formatted Status Request (1块) =====
--[p123]-- (1) Description
The host asks the NSR for the equipment mode, status, and processing state according to a predefined fixed format, and the NSR replies with this data.
(2) Transaction (Figure 5-18)
(3) Message Structure
S1,F5
S1,F6 Status data in specified format.
SFCD: Status format code.

===== [p50-50] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > (3) SType = 2: Select.rsp (1块) =====
--[p50]-- An HSMS message with SType 2 is a “Select Response” Control Message, used as the response to a Select.req Control message in the procedure for establishing HSMS communications.

===== [p17-17] SECTION 7 HEADER STRUCTURE > 7.7.2 Reply Messages (1块) =====
--[p17]-- The system bytes of the reply message are required to be the same as the system bytes of the corresponding primary message.

===== [p13-13] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.6 CHECKSUM (1块) =====
--[p13]-- The checksum is calculated as the numeric sum of the unsigned binary values of all the bytes after the length byte and before the checksum in a single block. The checksum is sent as 16 bits in two bytes following the last byte of the block data. The checksum is used by the receiver to check for transmission errors. The receiver performs the same checksum calculation on the received header and data. (When the checksums are the same, the transmission is recognized as successful.)

===== [p32-32] SECTION 3 SELECTED DEFINITIONS (2块) =====
--[p32]-- Connection: A logical link established between two entities on a TCP/IP LAN for the purpose of exchanging messages.
Entity: The applications associated with an endpoint (terminal) of a TCP/IP connection.
Local Entity: Relative to a particular endpoint of a connection, the local entity is the NSR.
Open Transaction: A transaction in progress.
Receiver: The HSMS Entity receiving a message.
Remote Entity: Relative to a particular connection endpoint, the remote entity is the host.
--[p32]-- Remote Entity: Relative to a particular connection endpoint, the remote entity is the host.
Session: A relationship established between two entities for the purpose of exchanging HSMS messages.

(3) Message Structure
$$ \begin{array} { r l } { { \mathsf { S } } 2 , { \mathsf { F } } 2 0 \quad } & { { } \angle { \mathsf { R A C } } > } \end{array} $$
RIC: Reset code.
RAC: Reset acknowledge report.

===== [p34-34] SECTION 5 OVERVIEW OF HSMS STATES > HSMS Not Selected (1块) =====
--[p34]-- A substate of CONNECTED in which no HSMS session has been established or any previously established HSMS session has ended.

===== [p47-47] SECTION 8 HSMS MESSAGE FORMAT > Header Byte 2 (1块) =====
--[p47]-- This header byte is used in different ways for different HSMS messages. For Control Messages it contains zero or a status code. For a Data Message whose PType = 0, it contains the W-Bit and SECS Stream.

===== [p138-138] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.8.5 Example of Data Set Transfers (1块) =====
--[p138]-- A scenario for a typical Stream 13 transaction is described below (Figures 5-34 and 5-35). The receiving system sends an S13,F3 message to open a data set for reading. Information is sent from the sending to the receiving system by a series of S13,F5 messages. Finally, the data set is closed by sending an S13,F7 message.

===== [p12-12] SECTION 6 BLOCK TRANSFER PROTOCOL > 6.2.1 Inter-Character Timeout, T1 (1块) =====
--[p12]-- In the SECS-I specification, time is monitored on an inter-character basis. In this specification, time is monitored for an individual block, with time limited between receipt of the first character of the block and until the receipt of the last character of the block.
The value of the block supervision timer is calculated according to the following formula:
Block supervision timer value = MAXIMUM {(Length × T1), 2} (unit: sec.)
Length: message length
T1: inter-character timer value

===== [p311-311] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > The NSR tells the host whether the process program can be downloaded (Table 2-65). (1块) =====
--[p311 [表]]-- Table 2-65 S7F2 Message Format
S, HOST <— NSR
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 13 | Fixed length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | 0702H | S7F2 | W-bit = 0
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System bytes | System bytes in primary message
Text | 12 | IH format | 21H | binary
13 | IH length | 1
14 | PPGNT | Process Program Grant Status (Note 1)
15~16 | Checksum
NOTE

===== [p214-214] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.1 S\*F0 ABORT TRANSACTION (1块) =====
--[p214 [表]]-- Used to abort an ongoing transaction. This message is sent as a response in multiblock or in reply to a primary message (Table 2-1).
Table 2-1 S\*F0 Message Format
S, HOST <— NSR
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 10 | Fixed length (header only)
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | XX00H | XX:stream number ofprimary message | W-bit = 0
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System bytes | System bytes in primary message.
12~13 | Checksum

===== [p145-145] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.9.7 System Parameter Request (1块) =====
--[p145]-- (1) Description
This function enables the host to request the values of equipment constants (set system parameters) from the NSR, which replies with the corresponding data. These equipment constants (set system parameters) reflect values set using the SET System Parameters command.
(2) Transaction (Figure 5-44)
(3) Message Structure S2,F71 L,n 1. n. S2,F72 L,n 1. n.
ECID: Equipment constant (set system parameter) ID.
ECV: Equipment constant (set system parameter) value.

===== [p311-311] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > The NSR tells the host whether the process program can be downloaded (Table 2-65). > 1. PPGNT: (1块) =====
--[p311]-- 0: Download granted
2: Space unavailable
3: Invalid PPID
64: Not granted; currently in use
66: Not granted; illegal mode
128: Internal system error
2. These items represent the PPBODY byte length indicated by LENGTH in S7F1, S7F65, or S7F67 (PPI). A zero-length item is granted as an exception.

===== [p122-122] SECTION 5 DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF INTERFACE FUNCTIONS > 5.3.2 Selected Equipment Status Request (1块) =====
--[p122]-- (1) Description
The host asks the NSR for the values of selected status variables, and the NSR replies with the requested data.
(2) Transaction (Figure 5-17)
(3) Message Structure S1,F3 L,n 1. n.
S1,F4 L,n 1. n.
SVID: Status variable ID. SV: Status variable data.

===== [p318-318] SECTION 2 HOST-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > 2.43 S7F66 RESERVED PROCESS PROGRAM LOAD GRANT (PPG) (1块) =====
--[p318 [表]]-- The NSR responds, to the host, regarding whether the process program can be downloaded (Table 2-73).
Table 2-73 S7F66 Message Format S, HOST <— NSR
Byte | Item | Data | Description
1 | Message length | 13 | Fixed length
Header | 2~3 | Device ID | R-bit = 1
4~5 | Message ID | 0742H | S7F66 | W-bit = 0
6~7 | Block No. | 8001H | E-bit = 1
8~11 | System bytes | System bytes in primary message
Text | 12 | IH format | 21H | binary
13 | IH length | 1
14 | PPGNT | Process program grant status (Note 1)
Byte | Item | Data | Description
15~16 | Checksum

===== [p182-182] SECTION 6 NSR COMMUNICATION MODES > 9.5 RETICLE POD LOAD/UNLOAD INSTRUCTION (1块) =====
--[p182]-- The Reticle POD command is available only on NSRs equipped with SMIF Reticle Loader. In a standard configuration (no SMIF Reticle Loader), requesting execution of this command will result in an error.

===== [p353-353] SECTION 3 NSR-INITIATED TRANSACTIONS > NOTE (1块) =====
--[p353]-- 1. This string is the diagnostic message received in S2F25 (LDR).

===== [p230-230] APPENDIX C DISCRETE DATA AND CORRESPONDING ITEMS IN THE MCSV WINDOWS > (1) ALCD (Alarm code) (4块) =====
--[p230]-- 7:Attention flags
64: Recoverable error; waiting for operator assist (foreground processing)
65: Recoverable error; waiting for operator assist (background processing)
66: Irrecoverable error (background processing)
67:Warning about equipment status (background processing)
70: Recoverable error; waiting for operator assist (extra processing)
71: Irrecoverable error (extra processing)
72:Warning about equipment status (extra processing)
“Foreground," “background," and “extra processing” are defined as follows:
--[p230]-- 72:Warning about equipment status (extra processing)
• Foreground processing: Processing of the first command launched from a condition in which no commands are running.
• Background processing: Processing of the second command launched from a condition in which no commands are running, with processing of the first command still under way.
• Extra processing: Processing of all the commands launched during ongoing foreground and background processing.
(2) ALID (Alarm ID)
This item identifies the alarm.
--[p230]-- This item identifies the alarm.
If ALCD = 5, 6, 64 to 67, or 70 to 72, it indicates the NSR error number.
--[p230]-- This code defines an alarm classification parameter one byte in length.
Bit 8 (MSB) = 1 : Alarm condition occurred (alarm set)
Bit 8 (MSB) = 0 : Alarm condition cleared (alarm cleared)
Bits 1 to 7: Alarm category code:
1:Condition may be dangerous to people
2:Condition may harm equipment
3:Parameter control alarm
4:Parameter control error
5:Irrecoverable error (foreground processing)
6:Warning about equipment status (foreground processing)
7:Attention flags
8:Data integrity not guaranteed

===== [p231-231] APPENDIX C DISCRETE DATA AND CORRESPONDING ITEMS IN THE MCSV WINDOWS > (3) ALTX (Alarm Text) (3块) =====
--[p231]-- This provides the alarm message indicated by up to 40 alphanumeric characters. Three types of alarm text message exist. The exact type reported depends on the setting of Alarm Message Switch (o). However, “Text” is reported for a Category 7.
The alarm text messages are as follows:
Text: Error information reported in up to 40 alphanumeric characters. This is identical to the error screen message code displayed on the NSR workstation monitor.
--[p231]-- Detail-2: Reports detailed error code. Each value in this code (shown in hexadecimal) is represented by ASCll characters and separated by semicolons (;) according to the following format (byte lengths are as shown):
Command name; Error code; Unit code; Detail-1; Detail-2
Number of bytes:
6 | 8 | 2 8 | 8 (36 bytes in total)
This information corresponds to the message code displayed on the error screen on the NSR workstation monitor (Figure 2-2).
--[p231]-- This is identical to the error screen message code displayed on the NSR workstation monitor.
Detail: Error information reported in detailed error code. Each value in this code (as indicated in hexadecimal) is represented by four ASCIl bytes, separated by semicolons (;), in the following format:
Command ID; Module ID (not used); Detail-1; Detail-2; Detail-3; Detail-4; Detail-5; Detail-6;
Number of bytes: | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 (39 bytes in total)
This message corresponds to information displayed in the error screen on the NSR workstation monitor (Figure 2-1).

===== [p126-126] SECTION 6 EVENT REPORT > 6.1 EVENT REPORT OVERVIEW (1块) =====
--[p126]-- Event reporting is a capability that provides valid, specified information as a unit to the host when an event occurs in the NSR. This information (reporting data) is reported in an S6,F11 message. This section describes the structure, formats, length, and contents of the S6,F11 message.
Structure: L,3 1. 2. 3. L,a 1.L,2 1. 2. L,b 1. : b. a. L,2 1. 2. L,c 1. : C.
• (Data ID)
An ID used in linking to Multiblock Data Send Inquire/Grant (S6,F5/F6) messages.
• (Collection Event ID)
Data type and event identifier. This corresponds to the first layer of the hierarchical data structure.

===== [p37-39] NOTES > 3. Load origin slot: (2块) =====
--[p37 [表]]-- If the load origin carrier is set to “Right carrier," "Left carrier,” or “Extra carrier,” the number is 1 to 26. The setting is zero for “Line.”
DATAID | WFPRED | Wafer Processing End Report (Note 1)

===== [p228-228] APPENDIX C DISCRETE DATA AND CORRESPONDING ITEMS IN THE MCSV WINDOWS > SECTION 1 INTRODUCTION (2块) =====
--[p228]-- An alarm is reported by the NSR (to the host) when the NSR during operation has generates an error or requires operator intervention, or when such a state is cleared. An alarm report is made by the S5F1/F2 transaction (Alarm Report Send/Acknowledge) according to the NSR/OCSV Communication Specification (based on SECS-II).
This Alarm Report Summary defines the contents of the items comprising the S5F1 message initiated by the NSR. These items are:
(1) ALCD (Alarm code)
(2) ALID (Alarm ID)
--[p228]-- (1) ALCD (Alarm code)
(3) ALTX (ALARM text)

===== [p37-37] NOTES > 2. Load origin carrier: (1块) =====
--[p37]-- 1: Right carrier
2: Left carrier
3: Extra carrier
10: Line

===== [p127-127] SECTION 6 EVENT REPORT > 6.2 DATAID DETAILS (1块) =====
--[p127]-- This data is the ID used in linking to Multiblock Data Send Inquire/Grant (S6,F5/F6) messages. The value of the DATAID is determined not by a specific event but in the following fashion:
(1) The value is “1” when an event is reported for the first time after system startup.
(2) This integer is incremented each time an event is reported, up to a maximum value of 65,536. After the maximum is reached, the value resets to “1."

===== [p131-133] SECTION 6 EVENT REPORT > NOTES (2块) =====
--[p133]-- 1. The items (for each RPTID) are appeared in this table in the order of the reports to the host.
2. This event report is used only in the job reservation function.
--[p131]-- 1.For details on the format corresponding to each number see Section 6.6.
2. This event report takes place only when the S2,F41/F42 (Host Command Send/Acknowledge) made a request to start operation but the state failed to change to “Execute."(If the state had successfully changed to Execute, a CEID 1100H would have occurred.)
3. This event report is only used on the job reservation function.
4.This event report is not supported in NSR-i14/SF100 series.
5.
This event report is a function specific to NSR-EX systems.
6.This event report is a function specific to NSR-EX14 series.

(6) Name: Command End Status

≠0 | Abnormal end | -

===== [p143-143] SECTION 6 EVENT REPORT > 6.6 EVENT REPORT MESSAGE FORMAT (1块) =====
--[p143]-- Message S6,F11, which reports data when an event occurs, links a CEID defined for each event to a fixed RPTID (Report ID) and V (Variable data).
This section covers the structural contents of S6,F11 for each event.
Lightly shaded areas in the table indicate support only for static exposure systems while darkly shaded areas indicate support only for scanning exposure systems.
